- Choose n perhaps 6962. Use float64. Normalize sum to 1. Use FFT length maybe 16384 for n=6962 (next pow2 16384). Convolution real = ifft(|fft(a,pad)|^2). Need ensure accurate max. For n=6962, 16384 FFT. Computing 1000 evals ~ maybe 1-2s? fine.

- Objective function returns J and argmax positions (top peaks). To avoid numerical negative, max over real.

- Since scale invariant, set sum=1 after clipping. To keep values not too small? okay.

Optimization methods:

1. Projected subgradient/Lipschitz:
At each iteration, compute conv C and max M, active idx maybe top K (e.g., 1-20). Gradient for active: sum over active k of w_k * grad_k, with weights maybe (C_k - threshold) or all top. To minimize max, use subgradient of max = grad at one argmax, but better use superlevel: g = ∑_{k:C_k >= M - δ} grad_k. Then update a -= lr * g (Euclidean) and project to sum=1,a≥0. But quadratic nonconvex, may work. Need choose lr. Since C is scale quadratic, with sum=1, gradients scale O(1/n^2)? Let's estimate linear ramp sum=1: a~2i/n^2? Actually a_i≈2i/n^2, max a≈2/n. Gradient grad_k ~a[j] ~1/n. C~1/n. J~constant. Update a -= lr*grad; to change a by comparable 1/n, lr~1. But need careful. Use adaptive: lr = step_scale / norm(g). We'll evaluate.

2. Mirror descent multiplicative: a_new = a * exp(-lr * grad) for active weighted. Since a≥0 and sum constraint via normalization. This preserves positivity. It might be better. grad can be large at peaks. Use active weights. For J near 1.33, peaks one or few. Use top K.

Search optimization details:

Fast objective:
```
def fast_obj(a):
    a = np.asarray(a,float)
    s=a.sum()
    if s < 1e-12: return np.inf, np.nan, np.zeros...
    # normalize? For gradient, we can operate on normalized array.
    a_norm = a/s
    n=a_norm.size
    conv_len=2*n-1
    pad=1
    while pad < conv_len: pad <<=1
    F=np.fft.rfft(a_norm,pad)
    P=F.real*F.real+F.imag*F.imag
    c=np.fft.irfft(P,pad)[:conv_len]
    # c may have tiny negative, small numerical. max real.
    M=np.max(c)
    score=2*n*M
    # argmax index
    k=int(np.argmax(c))
    return float(score), float(M), k, c
```
But if we want sum not exactly 1 due clipping? We'll always normalize search arrays sum=1. For final, scale to max 1? If sum=1, max ~1/n ~1.4e-4, safe. evaluate_sequence will not clip. Sum=1 >0.01. Good. Return normalized linear? Values small. That's okay.

However, evaluate_sequence computes max_b = max(np.convolve(sequence, sequence)) and sum_a = np.sum(sequence). If sum=1 and max_b ~4/(3n) (~2e-4 for n=7000), score ~1.333. Numerical precision: np.convolve many small numbers sum to ~1, max ~2e-4, double precision fine. But evaluating final with direct conv may have accumulated error ~n*eps*1^2 ~1e-11, okay.

 Could scale sequence so max=1? Then sum~n/2 ~3500, conv max~? score same. Larger numbers may increase cancellation? Direct conv of positive no cancellation, but double sum ~1e7? If values up to1000, conv max ~? sum^2~1e7, max_b~1.33/2n * sum^2 ~? for max value 1000, sum~3.5e5? sum^2=1.2e11, max_b ~1e7, within double. But small values sum=1 maybe safer? g[j] = ∑_r w_r * a[k_r - j] (if 0<=k_r-j<n). Actually derivative of C_k = 2∑ a_i a_{k_i-i}; gradient wrt a_j = 2∑_{r} a[k_r-j]. But max of weighted sum? If minimizing average top, gradient as sum. We can omit factor2.

Map to vectorized: For each active k, add reversed slice.
```
def add_gradient(g, a, ks, ws):
   n=len(a)
   for idx,k in enumerate(ks):
      w=ws[idx]
      if k < n:
          g[:k+1] += w * a[k::-1]
      else:
          start=k-n+1
          g[start:] += w * a[n-1:k-n+:-1]
```
Check for k>=n: indices j from k-n+1 to n-1, a[k-j] ranges a[n-1] down to a[k-n+1]. So `a[n-1 : k-n : -1]` length n - (k-n+1)=2n-1-k. Yes.
For k<n, length k+1: a[k::-1].

Mirror descent update:
`a_new = a * np.exp(-lr * g)` if g nonnegative. Then normalize. To keep stable, clip exponent. lr choose based on g norm. Since a~1/n, g~number_active/n. For K=1, g~=a shifted ~1/n. lr * g ~ lr/n. For meaningful factor e^{-0.1}, lr~0.1n~700. We can set lr = step * n / max(g?) or `lr = step / np.max(g)` where step e.g. 0.1-1. If max(g)~1/n, lr~ n. Let's use `lr = step / max(1e-30, np.max(g))`. With step 0.1, exponent max 0.1. Good.

Multiplicative projected:
`a_new = a * exp(-lr * g)` (g nonnegative), clip/normalize. If g=0, unchanged. Use Adam-like scaling: maintain exp_mom? Simpler: `lr = rel_step / max(g)` with rel_step ~0.1-1.0. If active weights normalized, max g ~ max a. For stable, rel_step=0.05? Too slow. Use 0.5.

Actually gradient of ∑(C-T)^2 wrt a_j = ∑_k 2(C_k - T) * 2 a[k-j] (if using full derivative; constant 2). We can omit factors. This is a linear operator (convolution of weights with a). Can compute by FFT? weights w_k = C_k - T length 2n-1; gradient C? g_j = ∑_k w_k a[k-j]. This is cross-correlation of w and a, can compute via FFT. Nice! O(n log n). But w depends on C=conv(a,a). So each iteration: compute C via FFT, set w=C-T, compute g via FFT correlation. Then multiplicative update.

This is a powerful "flatten convolution" algorithm. Let's formulate precisely.

Implementation of flatten update:
```
def flatten_step(a, T, rel_step):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    C = conv(a)
    r = C - T
    # Compute g_j = sum_m a_m r_{j+m}
    # Use FFT conv of a_rev and r.
    P=1; while P < n + L -1: P<<=1
    Ar=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], P)
    Rr=np.fft.rfft(r, P)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(Ar*Rr, P)
    g = conv[n-1 : n-1+n]  # j=0..n-1
    # optionally center normalize? g can negative.
    maxg=np.max(np.abs(g))
    if maxg==0: return a, best
    lr = rel_step / maxg
    # To avoid large exponent clip
    exponent = -lr * g
    exponent = np.clip(exponent, -5, 5)
    a_new = a * np.exp(exponent)
    a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 0)
    s=a_new.sum()
    if s<1e-12: ...
    a_new/=s
    return a_new
```
But T average =1/L (~7e-5 for n=7000). residual values ~1/n. g magnitude? r ~1/n, a~1/n, sum n ->1/n. maxg maybe inconsistent. rel_step controls multiplier.

However, if r has negative sum? ∑ r =1 - L*T. If T=1/L, sum zero. Good. If T different, scaling shift. T=average ensures zero total residual, maybe better. Target max T=0.75/n > average. If set T=average, we aim flat at average. Good.

Let's analyze residual gradient from Fourier perspective. a_new multiplicative by exp(-lr * (r ⋆ a)). This resembles iterative reweighted least squares for feasibility. Could be unstable. Need step small.

We can combine with simulated annealing: run flatten steps from different initials, accept improvements. Periodically return best.

Let's design functions:

```
def _next_pow2(m):
    p=1
    while p<m: p <<=1
    return p

def _fft_conv(a, pad=None):
    n=a.size; conv_len=2*n-1
    if pad is None: pad=_next_pow2(conv_len)
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,pad)
    C=np.fft.irfft(F*F.conj(), pad)[:conv_len]
    return C

def _fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=0: return np.inf
    an=a/s
    C=_fft_conv(an)
    M=float(np.max(C))
    return float(2.0*a.size*max(0.0,M))
```
For score using FFT, C may have small negative due roundoff; max positive. Could also clip C to 0? Max positive unaffected.

`_score_and_grad(a)`:
- an normalize.
- C = _fft_conv(an)
- M=max(C)
- score=2nM
- optional average T = 1/(2n-1).
- residual r = C - T? Or C - target? We'll pass T.
- g computation:
```
def _residual_grad(an, r):
    n=an.size; L=2*n-1
    P=_next_pow2(n + L -1)
    Ar=np.fft.rfft(an[::-1], P)
    Rr=np.fft.rfft(r, P)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(Ar*Rr, P)
    return conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Check slice: a_rev length n, r length L, conv length n+L-1 =3n-1. For j=0, t=n-1: sum_i a_rev[i] r[n-1-i]; i=0..n-1 => a_{n-1-i} r[n-1-i]? That seems not j=0. Let's derive again.
We want g_j = ∑_{m=0}^{n-1} a_m r_{j+m}. Let a_rev[i]=a_{n-1-i}. Convolution conv[t]=∑_{i=0}^{n-1} a_rev[i] r[t-i] (assuming r index nonnegative). Set t = j+n-1. Then conv[j+n-1] = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1} a_{n-1-i} r[j+n-1-i]. Let m=n-1-i => i=n-1-m. r index = j+n-1-(n-1-m)=j+m. a_{n-1-i}=a_m. Sum m=0..n-1 a_m r[j+m]. Correct. So slice start n-1 length n. Good.
- Gradient of F = ∑ (C-T)^2 wrt a is 4*g. We can ignore 4.

`_flatten_update(a, T, rel_step, clip_exp)`:
- an=a/s
- C=_fft_conv(an)
- score=2n maxC
- r=C - T
- g=_residual_grad(an,r)
- maxg=np.max(np.abs(g)); if tiny return an,score
- lr=rel_step/maxg
- exp_arg=-lr*g
- clip
- an2=an*np.exp(exp_arg)
- an2=np.maximum(an2,0)
- s2=an2.sum(); if s2<=0 return an,score
- an2/=s2
- return an2, score (?) We should recompute score after update in caller.

But T choice: We can set T = 1/(2n-1) (average). Maybe target-dependent: T = target_J_current/(2n)? If current score > target, T= target/(2n) may be above average. Flattening to lower target could be good. But if current max below target, we want further lower; T= average is natural. We can mix T = alpha*current_max + (1-alpha)*average. Use `T = (C.max()*0.2 + average*0.8)`? Let's think. If T=average, gradient tries make all C equal average; at overpeaks positive, underpeaks negative. This is what we want. If current max is 0.7/n, average0.5/n, T=average; good. If current max badly 1/n, T=average; gradient strong to lower peaks and raise low. Good. Use T=average.

However, if C has many negative residuals at edges, g negative can increase a at positions contributing to edge lags, potentially creating boundary spikes. That's desired but could overshoot. Multiplicative with small step and acceptance controls.

We can also use smooth max objective with soft-top. Maybe simpler: top-lag flatten. But residual full may be efficient.

```
a = best.copy()

rel=0.5
for iter:
   a_new, score_new = flatten_update(a,T,rel)
   if score_new < best_score: accept; best=...
   else: try smaller rel or different T; occasionally random perturbation
```

Phase A implementation:
```
def _explore_parametric(deadline, n_list=None):
    best_score=np.inf; best_a=None; best_info=None
    rng=...
    for n in n_list:
       p_grid=np.arange(0.2,1.0,0.01) or 80 points
       for p in p_grid:
          for c in [1.0, 0.5]: # maybe only if time
             a=_power_seq(n,p,c)
             sc=_fast_score(a)
             if sc<best: store
       if time close break
    return best
```

`_local_flatten(a, start, deadline, rng, max_iter=?):`
- best_a=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a) maybe also evaluate direct? Use fast.
- current_a=a.copy()
- T=1/(2n-1)
- step=0.5
- fail=0
- iteration=0
- while time<deadline:

Pseudo:
```
def _flatten_trial(a, T, rel, clip=2.0):
    n=a.size
    C=_fft_conv(a)
    r=C - T
    g=_residual_grad(a,r)
    maxg=np.max(np.abs(g))
    if maxg <= 0: return a
    lr=rel/maxg
    arg=-lr*g
    arg=np.clip(arg,-clip,clip)
    new=a*np.exp(arg)
    new=np.maximum(new,0.0)
    s=new.sum()
    if s<=0: return a
    return new/s
```

```
def _local_improve(a0, deadline, verbose_prefix=""):
    n=a0.size
    best=a0.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a0)
    cur=a0.copy()
    step=0.2
    failures=0
    total_iter=0
    eval_every=20
    # T fixed average
    T_base=1.0/(2*n-1)
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        for mode in ['flatten','noise']:
           if time>deadline: break
           if mode flatten:
              T = T_base # maybe interpolation
              candidate = _flatten_trial(cur, T, step)
           else:
              sigma = 0.05 + 0.1*(failures%?)
              candidate = _random_block(cur, rng, sigma)
           sc = _fast_score(candidate)
           if sc < best_score:
               best=candidate; best_score=sc; cur=candidate
               failures=0
               step=min(1.0, step*1.1)
           else:
               failures +=1
               step=max(0.02, step*0.8)
               cur=best.copy() # revert
               if failures > 50:
                   # random restart around best
                   sigma=0.05
                   cur=_global_noise(best, rng, sigma)
                   failures=20
                   step=0.2
        total_iter +=2
        if total_iter % 100 ==0: print progress
        if failures >500:
             # try reinitialize from parametric? But function doesn't know params. Could just break or global noise.
             cur = _global_noise(best, rng, 0.02)
             failures=100
    return best,best_score
```

Helper functions:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _pow2_ge(m):
    p=1
    while p<m: p<<=1
    return p

def _fft_convolve(a, pad=None):
    n=int(a.size)
    conv_len=2*n-1
    if pad is None:
        pad=_pow2_ge(conv_len)
    f=np.fft.rfft(a,pad)
    c=np.fft.irfft(f*f.conj(),pad)[:conv_len]
    return c

def _normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <=0:
       return np.ones(a.size,dtype=np.float64)/a.size
    return a/s
```

`_fast_score(a)`:
```
def _fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a=_normalize(a)
    c=_fft_convolve(a)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m<0: m=0.0
    return float(2.0*int(a.size)*m)
```
For normalized sum1, if c max negative due tiny, 0.

`_score_and_conv(a)` maybe returns score, c.
```
def _score_conv(a):
    a=_normalize(a)
    c=_fft_convolve(a)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    score=float(2*a.size*m) if m>0 else 0.0
    return score,c,a
```

`_power_sequence(n,p,c)`:
```
def _power_sequence(n,p,c):
    x=np.arange(1.0, float(n)+1.0, dtype=np.float64) + max(0.0, c-1.0)
    # if c not 1? We want (i+c)^-p for i=0..: x=np.arange(0,n)+c
    # simpler: x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)+c
    if c <=0: c=1e-6
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)+float(c)
    # avoid zero
    x=np.maximum(x,1e-300)
    if p ==0: a=np.ones(n)
    else: a=np.exp(-p*np.log(x))  # better for p range
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: a=np.ones(n)/n
    else: a/=s
    return a
```
Using exp(-p log x) supports negative p? If p negative, -p log positive may overflow if x large and -p large. For p in [-1,2], safe. Could use `x ** (-p)` directly with np.power; okay. Use `a = x ** (-float(p))`. For p negative, increasing. For x up to 20000, p=-2 -> 4e8, okay. For p=2 -> small. No overflow. Use np.errstate.

`_make_param_candidate(info)`? We store n,p,c and reconstruct.

`_global_noise(a,rng,sigma)`:
```
def _global_noise(a,rng,sigma):
    n=a.size
    noise=rng.normal(0.0, max(1e-6, sigma), size=n)
    new=a*np.exp(noise)
    return _normalize(new)
```

`_block_noise(a,rng,sigma)`:
```
def _block_noise(a,rng,sigma):
    n=a.size
    if n<4: return _global_noise(a,rng,sigma)
    min_w=max(1,n//200)
    max_w=max(2,n//8)
    width=int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w))
    start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width)))
    new=a.copy()
    factor=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, max(1e-6,sigma)))
    new[start:start+width]*=factor
    if rng.random()<0.15:
       # mirror partial?
       new=0.5*(new+new[::-1])
    return _normalize(new)
```
Mirroring may symmetrize and hurt; avoid mirroring? One-sided asymmetry important. Do not mirror by default. Maybe 5% with small factor? Better no.

`_flatten_trial(a, target, rel, clip_exp)`:
```
def _flatten_trial(a,target,rel,clip_exp=2.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64); a=_normalize(a)
    n=a.size
    c=_fft_convolve(a)
    r=c-target
    # optionally scale negative residuals to avoid over-increasing low:
    # r = np.where(r>0, r, r*0.5)
    g=_residual_grad(a,r)
    maxg=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
    if not np.isfinite(maxg) or maxg <= 1e-300:
        return a
    lr=rel/maxg
    arg=-lr*g
    arg=np.clip(arg, -clip_exp, clip_exp)
    new=a*np.exp(arg)
    return _normalize(new)
```

`_residual_grad` as above. Need ensure r length exactly 2n-1. Could `_fft_convolve` returns length conv_len. Good.
```
def _residual_grad(a,r):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    if r.size != L: r=np.asarray(r); if r.size>L: r=r[:L]; else pad
    P=_pow2_ge(n+L-1)
    ar=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], P)
    rr=np.fft.rfft(r, P)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(ar*rr, P)
    start=n-1
    g=conv[start:start+n]
    return g
```
Could r contain small imaginary? c real. Good.

Maybe target=average=1/L. But `_flatten_trial` recomputes c; inefficient if we also evaluate new. Pair trial+score. We can combine `_flatten_trial_with_score` returning new, old_score? Not needed.

Local improve function top-level:

```
def _local_improve(a0, deadline, rng, step_init=0.2, verbose=False, prefix=""):
    a0=_normalize(a0)
    n=a0.size
    best=a0.copy()
    best_score=_fast_score(a0)
    cur=a0.copy()
    step=step_init
    fail=0
    target=1.0/(2.0*n-1.0)
    no_improve=0
    start_iter=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # try flatten
        cand = _flatten_trial(cur, target, step)
        sc = _fast_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best=cand; best_score=sc; cur=cand
            no_improve=0
            step=min(1.5, step*1.08)
        else:
            fail += 1
            step=max(0.02, step*0.85)
            cur=best.copy()
            if fail > 40:
                # noise
                sigma = 0.05 * (0.5 + 0.5*np.random? use rng)
                # scale sigma with time: start 0.1, decay
                sigma = 0.1
                cand = _block_noise(cur, rng, sigma)
                sc = _fast_score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best=cand; best_score=sc; cur=cand
                    fail=0; step=0.2
                else:
                    fail-=20
        # if no improvement for many, try global noise around best with smaller sigma
        if fail > 200:
            sigma=0.03
            cand=_global_noise(best,rng,sigma)
            sc=_fast_score(cand)
            if sc < best_score:
                best=cand; best_score=sc
            fail=50
            step=0.15
        if verbose and (fail%100==0 or ...): print
    return best,best_score
```

Local improve:

```
def _local_improve(a0, deadline, rng, verbose=False):
    a0 = _normalize_sequence(a0)
    best = a0.copy()
    best_score = _fast_score(best)
    cur = a0.copy()
    n = best.size
    target = 1.0 / float(2*n -1)
    step = 0.2
    fail = 0
    since_improve = 0
    sigma = 0.1
    iter_count=0
    next_report = time.monotonic()+20.0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        iter_count += 1
        # Flatten
        cand = _flatten_trial(cur, target, step)
        sc = _fast_score(cand)
        accepted=False
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = cand; best_score=sc; cur=cand
            accepted=True
            since_improve=0
            step = min(1.2, step*1.07)
            fail=0
        else:
            fail += 1
            step = max(0.02, step*0.85)
            cur = best.copy()
            # occasionally try alternate target
            if fail % 25 == 0:
                t2 = max(1e-15, min(target, (best_score/(2.0*n))*0.6))
                cand2 = _flatten_trial(best, t2, step)
                sc2 = _fast_score(cand2)
                if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score -1e-12:
                    best=cand2; best_score=sc2; cur=best; fail=0; step=min(1.0, step*1.2); continue
            if fail > 30:
                # noise
                if iter_count % 2 == 0:
                    candn = _block_noise(cur, rng, max(0.02, sigma))
                else:
                    candn = _global_noise(cur, rng, max(0.02, sigma*0.7))
                scn = _fast_score(candn)
                if np.isfinite(scn) and scn < best_score -1e-12:
                    best=candn; best_score=scn; cur=candn; fail=0
                    step = min(1.0, step*1.0) # reset moderate
                else:
                    if fail > 80:
                        sigma = max(0.02, sigma*0.8)
                    fail -= 20
        since_improve += 1
        if since_improve > 500:
            # more aggressive restart
            candr = _global_noise(best, rng, max(0.05, sigma))
            scr = _fast_score(candr)
            if np.isfinite(scr) and scr < best_score -1e-10:
                best=candr; best_score=scr
            since_improve=0
            sigma = max(0.02, sigma*0.9)
            fail=0
            step=0.15
        if verbose and time.monotonic() > next_report:
            print(prefix + " local best", best_score, "iter", iter_count, flush=True)
            next_report = time.monotonic()+30.0
    return best, best_score
```
This uses iter_count and fail. Might be okay.

But `_local_improve` uses `prefix` variable if not argument? Include prefix default.

Need pass prefix. Use argument `prefix=""`.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = start + total_budget - 5.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_a = None; best_score = np.inf; best_n=None; best_p=None; best_c=None

    # try parent
    try:
       par = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       if par.size>0 and np.isfinite(par).all():
           par = np.maximum(par,0)
           sp = _fast_score(par)
           if sp<best_score:
              best_score=sp; best_a=par; best_n=par.size
              print("parent score", sp)
    except Exception:
       pass

    # parametric search
    n_opts = [64,128,256,512,1024,2048,4096,6962,8192,16384]
    # maybe skip too many if time? We'll do all but break
    # We'll also store top_configs list for local.
    records=[]
    coarse_ps = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 26) # 0,0.04...1
    # Include p=0.5 exact
    # For each n:
    for n in n_opts:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 300.0: break  # leave time
       local_best_sc=np.inf; local_best_p=0.5; local_best_c=1.0
       for p in coarse_ps:
          a=_power_sequence(n,p,1.0)
          sc=_fast_score(a)
          if sc<local_best_sc:
              local_best_sc=sc; local_best_p=p
          if sc<best_score:
              best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy(); best_n=n; best_p=p; best_c=1.0
              records.append((sc,a.copy(),n,p,1.0))
       # refine p
       p0=local_best_p
       for radius, count, cs in [(0.12,41,(1.0,)), (0.04,31,(0.75,1.0,1.25)), (0.012,25,(0.75,0.875,1.0,1.125,1.5))]:
           ps=np.linspace(p0-radius, p0+radius, count)
           ps=ps[(ps>=-0.2)&(ps<=1.2)]
           for c in cs:
              for p in ps:
                 a=_power_sequence(n,p,c)
                 sc=_fast_score(a)
                 if sc<local_best_sc:
                     local_best_sc=sc; local_best_p=p; local_best_c=c
                 if sc<best_score:
                     best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy(); best_n=n; best_p=p; best_c=c
                     records.append((sc,a.copy(),n,p,c))
       # after n, maybe add local_best record even if not global
       # not necessary
    # optional explore other simple shapes: constant, sin? We can add a few.
```

Top update helper top-level:
```
def _update_top(records, score, n, p, c, k=8):
    if np.isinf(score): return records
    if len(records) < k:
       records.append((score,float(n),float(p),float(c)))
    else:
       worst=0; wsz=records[0][0]
       for i in range(1,len(records)):
          if records[i][0] > wsz: wz=records[i][0]; worst=i
       if score < wz:
          records[worst]=(score,float(n),float(p),float(c))
    return records
```

Detailed `_normalize_sequence`:
```
def _normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s
```
If input is scalar? ravel works.

`_fft_convolve`:
```
def _fft_convolve(a, pad=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    conv_len = 2*n-1
    if pad is None or pad < conv_len:
        pad = _pow2_ge(conv_len)
    f = np.fft.rfft(a, pad)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f * f.conjugate(), pad)
    return c[:conv_len]
```
f*f.conjugate() introduces complex multiply; use `np.abs(f)**2` maybe. `f * f.conj()` fine.

`_fast_score`:
```
def _fast_score(a):
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0: return np.inf
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    m = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m)
```

`_power_sequence`:
```
def _power_sequence(n,p,c):
    n = int(n)
    if n <=0: n=1
    c = float(c)
    if c <= 1e-12: c=1e-12
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + c
    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore', invalid='ignore'):
         a = np.power(x, -float(p))
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
         a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    else:
         a = a / s
    return a
```
If p very negative and n large, x^-p = x^positive overflow? p range limited, safe. If overflow, set nonfinite to 0, but that may spoil; p search avoids.

Maybe for p negative large, better compute logs? But p range -0.5 to0.9, no overflow.

`_global_noise`:
```
def _global_noise(a, rng, sigma):
   a = _normalize_sequence(a)
   n = a.size
   if n == 0: return a
   sigma = float(max(1e-6, sigma))
   noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=n)
   new = a * np.exp(noise)
   return _normalize_sequence(new)
```

`_block_noise`:
```
def _block_noise(a, rng, sigma):
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    if n < 8:
        return _global_noise(a, rng, sigma)
    min_w = max(2, n // 200)
    max_w = max(min_w + 1, n // 8)
    width = int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w))
    if width <=0: width=1
    if width >= n: width = n
    hi = max(0, n - width)
    start = int(rng.integers(0, hi + 1)) if hi > 0 else 0
    new = a.copy()
    sigma = float(max(1e-6, sigma))
    factor = float(rng.normal(0.0, sigma))
    new[start:start+width] *= np.exp(factor)
    return _normalize_sequence(new)
```

`_residual_grad` with try:
```
def _residual_grad(a, r):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    L = 2*n - 1
    r = np.asarray(r, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if r.size < L:
        tmp = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        tmp[:r.size] = r
        r = tmp
    else:
        r = r[:L]
    r = np.where(np.isfinite(r), r, 0.0)
    P = _pow2_ge(n + L - 1)
    ar = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], P)
    rr = np.fft.rfft(r, P)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(ar * rr, P)
    return conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```

`_flatten_trial` with `neg_scale` parameter:
```
def _flatten_trial(a, target, rel, clip_exp=2.0, neg_scale=0.6):
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    if target is None or not np.isfinite(target):
       target = 1.0 / float(2*n -1)
    r = c - float(target)
    # damp negative residuals
    pos = r > 0.0
    r = np.where(pos, r, neg_scale * r)
    g = _residual_grad(a, r)
    maxg = float(np.max(np.abs(g))) if g.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(maxg) or maxg <= 1e-300:
        return a
    lr = float(rel) / maxg
    arg = -lr * g
    arg = np.clip(arg, -float(clip_exp), float(clip_exp))
    new = a * np.exp(arg)
    return _normalize_sequence(new)
```
Does `np.where(pos, r, neg_scale*r)` allocate, fine. `neg_scale*r` computed both branches? It's fine.

`_update_top` as top-level:
```
def _update_top(records, score, n, p, c):
    if not np.isfinite(score): return records
    score = float(score); n=int(n); p=float(p); c=float(c)
    if len(records) < 8:
        records.append((score,n,p,c))
    else:
        worst_idx=0; worst_score=records[0][0]
        for i in range(1,len(records)):
            if records[i][0] > worst_score:
                worst_score=records[i][0]; worst_idx=i
        if score < worst_score:
            records[worst_idx]=(score,n,p,c)
    return records
```

Direct score:
```
def _direct_score(a):
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    try:
        vals = [float(x) for x in a]
        return float(evaluate_sequence(vals))
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
```
Direct for final only.

Let's write `_local_improve` more robust:

```
def _local_improve(a0, deadline, rng, prefix=""):
    a0 = _normalize_sequence(a0)
    if a0.size == 0: return a0, np.inf
    best = a0.copy()
    best_score = _fast_score(best)
    cur = a0.copy()
    n = int(best.size)
    target = 1.0 / float(2*n - 1)
    step = 0.25
    neg_step = 0.6
    sigma = 0.08
    fail = 0
    since_improve = 0
    iter_count = 0
    next_print = time.monotonic() + 15.0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        iter_count += 1
        # try several flatten step sizes maybe
        candidate = _flatten_trial(cur, target, step, 2.0, neg_step)
        sc = _fast_score(candidate)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = candidate; best_score = sc; cur = candidate
            fail = 0; since_improve = 0
            step = min(1.5, step * 1.08)
            # sometimes reduce neg_scale? not
        else:
            fail += 1
            step = max(0.02, step * 0.82)
            cur = best.copy()
            if fail % 20 == 0:
                # alternate target: closer to current peak
                t_alt = max(1e-15, min(target * 2.0, (best_score / (2.0 * n)) * 0.75))
                cand2 = _flatten_trial(best, t_alt, step, 2.0, neg_step)
                sc2 = _fast_score(cand2)
                if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best = cand2; best_score = sc2; cur = best
                    fail = 0; since_improve = 0; step = min(1.0, step * 1.2); continue
            if fail > 35:
                # noise
                if iter_count % 2 == 0:
                   candn = _block_noise(best, rng, max(0.02, sigma))
                else:
                   candn = _global_noise(best, rng, max(0.015, sigma * 0.8))
                scn = _fast_score(candn)
                if np.isfinite(scn) and scn < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best = candn; best_score = scn; cur = candn
                    fail = 0; since_improve = 0
                    step = min(1.0, max(0.2, step * 1.5))
                else:
                    if fail > 120:
                        sigma = max(0.015, sigma * 0.85)
                    fail -= 25
        since_improve += 1
        if since_improve > 800:
            candr = _global_noise(best, rng, max(0.04, sigma))
            scr = _fast_score(candr)
            if np.isfinite(scr) and scr < best_score - 1e-10:
                best = candr; best_score = scr
            since_improve = 0
            fail = 0
            step = 0.2
            sigma = max(0.02, sigma * 0.9)
        if time.monotonic() > next_print:
            print(prefix, "local best", best_score, "iter", iter_count, "step", step, "fail", fail, flush=True)
            next_print = time.monotonic() + 30.0
    return best, best_score
```

This may print a lot; okay. Maybe prefix with n.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        budget=1800.0
    deadline = start + budget - 5.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_score = np.inf
    best_a = None
    records = []

    # parent
    try:
        par = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if par.size > 0:
            par = np.where(np.isfinite(par), par, 0.0)
            par=par>=0
            sp=_fast_score(par)
            if sp < best_score:
                best_score=sp; best_a=par.copy()
                print("height_sequence_1 score", sp, flush=True)
    except Exception:
        pass

    param_deadline = start + min(240.0, max(60.0, budget*0.15))
    n_opts = [64,128,256,512,1024,2048,4096,6962,8192,16384]
    base_ps = np.linspace(-0.35, 0.95, 27)
    for n in n_opts:
        if time.monotonic() > param_deadline:
            break
        print("param n", n, flush=True)
        local_best_score = np.inf
        local_best_p = 0.5
        local_best_c = 1.0
        for p in base_ps:
            a = _power_sequence(n, p, 1.0)
            sc = _fast_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                if sc < local_best_score:
                    local_best_score = sc; local_best_p = float(p)
                if sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc; best_a = a.copy()
                _update_top(records, sc, n, p, 1.0)
        # refine p with c=1
        p0 = local_best_p
        for radius, count in [(0.12, 33), (0.03, 25)]:
            lo = max(-0.6, p0 - radius)
            hi = min(1.2, p0 + radius)
            if hi <= lo: continue
            ps = np.linspace(lo, hi, count)
            for p in ps:
                if time.monotonic() > param_deadline: break
                a = _power_sequence(n, p, 1.0)
                sc = _fast_score(a)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    if sc < local_best_score:
                        local_best_score = sc; local_best_p = float(p)
                    if sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc; best_a = a.copy()
                    _update_top(records, sc, n, p, 1.0)
        # refine c around best p
        if time.monotonic() < param_deadline:
            p1 = local_best_p
            lo = max(-0.6, p1 - 0.03); hi = min(1.2, p1 + 0.03)
            ps = np.linspace(lo, hi, 21)
            for c in [0.5,0.75,1.0,1.25,1.5,2.0]:
                for p in ps:
                    if time.monotonic() > param_deadline: break
                    a = _power_sequence(n, p, c)
                    sc = _fast_score(a)
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        if sc < local_best_score:
                            local_best_score = sc; local_best_p = float(p); local_best_c = float(c)
                        if sc < best_score:
                            best_score = sc; best_a = a.copy()
                        _update_top(records, sc, n, p, c)
                if time.monotonic() > param_deadline: break
        print(" n", n, "local power best", local_best_score, flush=True)

    # Ensure records has best if any
    if not records:
        if best_a is not None and best_a.size >0:
            records.append((best_score, int(best_a.size), 0.5, 1.0))
        else:
            a = _power_sequence(1024, 0.51, 1.0)
            best_score = _fast_score(a); best_a = a.copy(); records.append((best_score,1024,0.51,1.0))

    # local phase
    local_configs = []
    seen_ns = []
    for rec in sorted(records):
        nrec = int(rec[1])
        if nrec in seen_ns:
            continue
        local_configs.append(rec)
        seen_ns.append(nrec)
        if len(local_configs) >= 3:
            break
    # ensure best a's n included? if best_a not from records? add
    if best_a is not None:
        nb = int(best_a.size)
        if nb not in [int(r[1]) for r in local_configs]:
            local_configs.insert(0, (best_score, nb, local_configs[0][2] if local_configs else 0.5, 1.0)) # p unknown; better reconstruct? Hmm.
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time


def _pow2_ge(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    p = 1
    m = int(m)
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def _normalize_sequence(a):
    """Clip to nonnegative finite values and normalize sum to one."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def _fft_convolve(a, pad=None):
    """Full linear convolution via FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    conv_len = 2 * n - 1
    if pad is None or pad < conv_len:
        pad = _pow2_ge(conv_len)
    f = np.fft.rfft(a, pad)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f * f.conjugate(), pad)
    return c[:conv_len]


def _fast_score(a):
    """Fast proxy for evaluate_sequence using FFT convolution."""
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    m = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def _power_sequence(n, p, c):
    """Sequence a_i = (i + c)**(-p), normalized, c > 0."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        n = 1
    c = float(c)
    if c <= 1e-12:
        c = 1e-12
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + c
    with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
        a = np.power(x, -float(p))
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    else:
        a = a / s
    return a


def _global_noise(a, rng, sigma):
    """Multiplicative global noise."""
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    sigma = float(max(1e-6, sigma))
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=n)
    new = a * np.exp(noise)
    return _normalize_sequence(new)


def _block_noise(a, rng, sigma):
    """Multiplicative perturbation on a random block."""
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 8:
        return _global_noise(a, rng, sigma)
    min_w = max(2, n // 200)
    max_w = max(min_w + 1, n // 8)
    width = int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w))
    if width <= 0:
        width = 1
    if width >= n:
        width = n
    hi = max(0, n - width)
    start = int(rng.integers(0, hi + 1)) if hi > 0 else 0
    new = a.copy()
    sigma = float(max(1e-6, sigma))
    factor = float(rng.normal(0.0, sigma))
    new[start:start + width] *= np.exp(factor)
    return _normalize_sequence(new)


def _residual_grad(a, r):
    """
    Gradient-like field g_j = sum_m a_m r_{j+m} for r indexed 0..2n-2.
    This is the derivative of sum_k (C_k - T)^2 up to an irrelevant factor.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    r = np.asarray(r, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if r.size < L:
        tmp = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        tmp[:r.size] = r
        r = tmp
    else:
        r = r[:L]
    r = np.where(np.isfinite(r), r, 0.0)
    P = _pow2_ge(n + L - 1)
    ar = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], P)
    rr = np.fft.rfft(r, P)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(ar * rr, P)
    return conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n]


def _flatten_trial(a, target, rel, clip_exp=2.0, neg_scale=0.6):
    """
    Multiplicative update trying to flatten C_k around target.
    Positive residuals are treated more strongly than negative residuals.
    """
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    if target is None or not np.isfinite(target):
        target = 1.0 / float(2 * n - 1)
    r = c - float(target)
    pos = r > 0.0
    r = np.where(pos, r, float(neg_scale) * r)
    g = _residual_grad(a, r)
    maxg = float(np.max(np.abs(g))) if g.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(maxg) or maxg <= 1e-300:
        return a
    lr = float(rel) / maxg
    arg = -lr * g
    arg = np.clip(arg, -float(clip_exp), float(clip_exp))
    new = a * np.exp(arg)
    return _normalize_sequence(new)


def _update_top(records, score, n, p, c):
    """Maintain a small list of best parametric candidates."""
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return records
    score = float(score)
    n = int(n)
    p = float(p)
    c = float(c)
    if len(records) < 8:
        records.append((score, n, p, c))
    else:
        worst_idx = 0
        worst_score = records[0][0]
        for i in range(1, len(records)):
            if records[i][0] > worst_score:
                worst_score = records[i][0]
                worst_idx = i
        if score < worst_score:
            records[worst_idx] = (score, n, p, c)
    return records


def _local_improve(a0, deadline, rng, prefix=""):
    """
    Local search: flatten-convolution multiplicative updates plus random
    multiplicative perturbations when flattening stagnates.
    """
    a0 = _normalize_sequence(a0)
    if a0.size == 0:
        return a0, np.inf
    best = a0.copy()
    best_score = _fast_score(best)
    cur = a0.copy()
    n = int(best.size)
    target = 1.0 / float(2 * n - 1)
    step = 0.25
    neg_scale = 0.6
    sigma = 0.08
    fail = 0
    since_improve = 0
    iter_count = 0
    next_print = time.monotonic() + 15.0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        iter_count += 1

        candidate = _flatten_trial(cur, target, step, 2.0, neg_scale)
        sc = _fast_score(candidate)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = candidate
            best_score = sc
            cur = candidate
            fail = 0
            since_improve = 0
            step = min(1.5, step * 1.08)
        else:
            fail += 1
            step = max(0.02, step * 0.82)
            cur = best.copy()

            if fail % 20 == 0:
                t_alt = max(1e-15, min(2.0 * target, (best_score / (2.0 * n)) * 0.75))
                cand2 = _flatten_trial(best, t_alt, step, 2.0, neg_scale)
                sc2 = _fast_score(cand2)
                if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best = cand2
                    best_score = sc2
                    cur = best
                    fail = 0
                    since_improve = 0
                    step = min(1.0, step * 1.2)
                    continue

            if fail > 35:
                if iter_count % 2 == 0:
                    candn = _block_noise(best, rng, max(0.02, sigma))
                else:
                    candn = _global_noise(best, rng, max(0.015, sigma * 0.8))
                scn = _fast_score(candn)
                if np.isfinite(scn) and scn < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best = candn
                    best_score = scn
                    cur = candn
                    fail = 0
                    since_improve = 0
                    step = min(1.0, max(0.2, step * 1.5))
                else:
                    if fail > 120:
                        sigma = max(0.015, sigma * 0.85)
                    fail -= 25

        since_improve += 1
        if since_improve > 800:
            candr = _global_noise(best, rng, max(0.04, sigma))
            scr = _fast_score(candr)
            if np.isfinite(scr) and scr < best_score - 1e-10:
                best = candr
                best_score = scr
            since_improve = 0
            fail = 0
            step = 0.2
            sigma = max(0.02, sigma * 0.9)

        if time.monotonic() > next_print:
            print(prefix, "local best", best_score, "iter", iter_count,
                  "step", step, "fail", fail, flush=True)
            next_print = time.monotonic() + 30.0

    return best, best_score


def _direct_score(a):
    """Call the official evaluator if available."""
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    try:
        vals = [float(x) for x in a]
        return float(evaluate_sequence(vals))
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for low-peak nonnegative convolution sequences.

    Strategy:
      1. Search a strong 1-parameter family a_i = (i+c)^(-p) over several
         lengths and shift exponents.
      2. From the best parametric starts, run a local multiplicative
         "convolution flattening" optimizer with random perturbations.
      3. Verify final candidates with the official evaluator and return the
         lowest valid sequence.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0
    reserve = min(45.0, max(5.0, budget * 0.05))
    deadline = start + budget - reserve
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_score = np.inf
    best_a = None
    best_n = 1024
    best_p = 0.51
    best_c = 1.0
    records = []

    # Include the supplied construction if it is useful.
    try:
        par = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if par.size > 0:
            par = np.where(np.isfinite(par), par, 0.0)
            par = np.maximum(par, 0.0)
            sp = _fast_score(par)
            if np.isfinite(sp) and sp < best_score:
                best_score = sp
                best_a = par.copy()
                best_n = int(par.size)
                best_p = 0.5
                best_c = 1.0
                print("parent score", sp, "n", best_n, flush=True)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Phase A: parametric power-law search.
    param_deadline = start + min(240.0, max(30.0, (deadline - start) * 0.2))
    n_opts = [64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 6962, 8192, 16384]
    base_ps = np.linspace(-0.35, 0.95, 27)

    for n in n_opts:
        if time.monotonic() > param_deadline:
            break
        print("param search n =", n, flush=True)
        local_best_score = np.inf
        local_best_p = 0.5
        local_best_c = 1.0

        for p in base_ps:
            a = _power_sequence(n, p, 1.0)
            sc = _fast_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                if sc < local_best_score:
                    local_best_score = sc
                    local_best_p = float(p)
                if sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_a = a.copy()
                    best_n = n
                    best_p = float(p)
                    best_c = 1.0
                _update_top(records, sc, n, p, 1.0)

        # Refine exponent around the best coarse value.
        p0 = local_best_p
        for radius, count in [(0.12, 33), (0.03, 25)]:
            lo = max(-0.6, p0 - radius)
            hi = min(1.2, p0 + radius)
            if hi <= lo:
                continue
            ps = np.linspace(lo, hi, count)
            for p in ps:
                if time.monotonic() > param_deadline:
                    break
                a = _power_sequence(n, p, 1.0)
                sc = _fast_score(a)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    if sc < local_best_score:
                        local_best_score = sc
                        local_best_p = float(p)
                    if sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc
                        best_a = a.copy()
                        best_n = n
                        best_p = float(p)
                        best_c = 1.0
                    _update_top(records, sc, n, p, 1.0)
            if time.monotonic() > param_deadline:
                break

        # Refine shift c around the best exponent.
        if time.monotonic() < param_deadline:
            p1 = local_best_p
            lo = max(-0.6, p1 - 0.03)
            hi = min(1.2, p1 + 0.03)
            ps = np.linspace(lo, hi, 21)
            for c in [0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0]:
                for p in ps:
                    if time.monotonic() > param_deadline:
                        break
                    a = _power_sequence(n, p, c)
                    sc = _fast_score(a)
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        if sc < local_best_score:
                            local_best_score = sc
                            local_best_p = float(p)
                            local_best_c = float(c)
                        if sc < best_score:
                            best_score = sc
                            best_a = a.copy()
                            best_n = n
                            best_p = float(p)
                            best_c = float(c)
                        _update_top(records, sc, n, p, c)
                if time.monotonic() > param_deadline:
                    break

        print(" n =", n, "local power best =", local_best_score, flush=True)

    if not records:
        if best_a is not None and best_a.size > 0:
            records.append((best_score, best_n, best_p, best_c))
        else:
            best_a = _power_sequence(1024, 0.51, 1.0)
            best_score = _fast_score(best_a)
            best_n = 1024
            best_p = 0.51
            best_c = 1.0
            records.append((best_score, best_n, best_p, best_c))

    # Phase B: local improvement from a few good parametric starts.
    local_configs = []
    used_ns = []
    if best_a is not None:
        local_configs.append((float(best_score), int(best_n), float(best_p), float(best_c)))
        used_ns.append(int(best_n))
    for rec in sorted(records):
        nrec = int(rec[1])
        if nrec in used_ns:
            continue
        local_configs.append((float(rec[0]), nrec, float(rec[2]), float(rec[3])))
        used_ns.append(nrec)
        if len(local_configs) >= 3:
            break

    if local_configs:
        t0 = time.monotonic()
        rem = max(0.0, deadline - t0)
        if len(local_configs) == 1:
            ends = [deadline]
        elif len(local_configs) == 2:
            ends = [t0 + 0.70 * rem, deadline]
        else:
            ends = [t0 + 0.55 * rem, t0 + 0.82 * rem, deadline]

        for i, rec in enumerate(local_configs):
            sc0, n0, p0, c0 = rec
            if i >= len(ends):
                end = deadline
            else:
                end = ends[i]
            if time.monotonic() >= end - 2.0:
                break
            a0 = _power_sequence(int(n0), float(p0), float(c0))
            sc0 = _fast_score(a0)
            if np.isfinite(sc0):
                if sc0 < best_score:
                    best_score = sc0
                    best_a = a0.copy()
                    best_n = int(n0)
                    best_p = float(p0)
                    best_c = float(c0)
                _update_top(records, sc0, int(n0), float(p0), float(c0))
            prefix = "n=%d p=%.4f c=%.4f | " % (int(n0), float(p0), float(c0))
            print("local start:", prefix, sc0, flush=True)
            a1, sc1 = _local_improve(a0, end, rng, prefix=prefix)
            sc1 = _fast_score(a1)
            if np.isfinite(sc1) and sc1 < best_score:
                best_score = sc1
                best_a = a1.copy()
                best_n = int(a1.size)
                # For local-improved nonparametric sequences exponent info is irrelevant.
                _update_top(records, sc1, int(a1.size), 0.5, 1.0)
            print("local end:", prefix, sc1, flush=True)

    # Final verification and selection. Prefer official direct score.
    final_candidates = []
    if best_a is not None:
        final_candidates.append(_normalize_sequence(best_a))
    for rec in sorted(records)[:3]:
        a = _power_sequence(int(rec[1]), float(rec[2]), float(rec[3]))
        final_candidates.append(_normalize_sequence(a))

    # If the best so far is still above the stated target, add a few safe
    # static fallbacks and choose the best among them.
    if (not np.isfinite(best_score)) or best_score > 1.5027:
        for nf in [128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048]:
            for pf in [0.48, 0.50, 0.52, 0.54, 0.56]:
                a = _power_sequence(nf, pf, 1.0)
                final_candidates.append(_normalize_sequence(a))

    final_a = None
    final_score = np.inf
    for a in final_candidates:
        if a is None or a.size == 0:
            continue
        a = _normalize_sequence(a)
        dscore = _direct_score(a)
        if not np.isfinite(dscore):
            dscore = _fast_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(dscore):
            if (dscore < final_score - 1e-7) or (
                final_a is None
            ) or (
                abs(dscore - final_score) <= 1e-7 and a.size < final_a.size
            ):
                final_score = dscore
                final_a = a.copy()

    if final_a is None:
        final_a = _power_sequence(1024, 0.51, 1.0)
        fallback_score = _direct_score(final_a)
        if not np.isfinite(fallback_score):
            fallback_score = _fast_score(final_a)
        if np.isfinite(fallback_score):
            final_score = fallback_score

    print("final chosen score (fast/direct best):", final_score,
          "n:", int(final_a.size), flush=True)
    return [float(x) for x in final_a]
```
